> ↔ **86 (no count change, P2 12, P3 74) on 2026-11-17**: narrowed the §2 gate *X5 cross-lane drill-down + StepInfo envelope* (MoSCoW S4; a §2 row is not counted) — lane **L3** of `archived-documents/plans-archive/2026-11-17-x5-stepinfo-envelope-plan.md` and **D9 = c** are built. The ingest lanes leave Step Receipts for every status: the flat lane in phases (`parse` with the reject sidecar count and the `LANE_FLAT` reason, `transform` with one `route:<rule>` / `reject:quarantined` / `reject:dropped` outlet per Decision Rule that moved rows — the route counts grounded in `DecisionRuleApplier.Result.moved` — and one `sink:<n>` per destination), the graph fork the same phases then every walked Step (`ConsignmentGraphRunner.run(…, observer)` with a `StepReceiptRecorder` in place of `NONE`, kept even when the walk throws). A parked sink reads `PARKED` with a `park` pointer; `DrainCommand` adds one `drain` receipt per parked sink. A failed Consignment's `pipeline.batch.failed` carries `failedStep` / `code`, and a failed at-rest job run now raises that Signal alone, never a `ConsignmentEvent`, so `on_signal: pipeline.commit` Jobs see no change. Deviation: ingest receipts carry the ingest Run id, not `''` (a retried Consignment would otherwise lose its second attempt to `DO NOTHING`). Storage unchanged (`consignment_steps`). Tests: `FlatVsGraphLaneParityTest`, `RouteIngestEndToEndTest`, `DecisionRuleWiringTest`, `PipelineJobRunnerTest`, each falsified. Review fixes the same day: a receipt's message is scrubbed of values (`FailureText.scrub`, now idempotent) in `StepReceipt.Diagnostics` on every lane; the job-lane Signal is raised by `JobService` once per Run that ends FAILED (none when cancelled or recovered by a retry; a refusal before the walk raises it with no `failedStep`); the ingest trail travels in the call context, not a map keyed by Consignment id; OpsMonitor's `batch_rerun` ignores a `job:`-sourced failure. Full gate NOT run.
> ↔ **86 (no count change, P2 12, P3 74) on 2026-11-17**: narrowed the §2 gate *X5 cross-lane drill-down + StepInfo envelope* (MoSCoW S4; a §2 row is not counted) — lane **L4**, the read route, is built: `GET /consignments/{consignmentId}` (`ConsignmentRoutes`, plan §3.7, D8 = a) returns one Consignment across lanes — status, member files, its Step Receipts, outputs (first 200), `upstream` / `downstream` job-run hops and its newest 50 Signals, with `known.{steps,runs,outputs}`; a store that cannot say leaves its key ABSENT (receipts off ⇒ no `steps` or `lanes`, never `[]`), 404 when no store knows the id. The hop is the new `run_id` join `DbConsignmentOutputStore.consignmentsOfRuns` (two queries for all readers: receipts first, then `consignment_outputs.run_id`). Every list is cut in its read with its true total beside it; the ledger row is found by id (`StatusStore.consignment`, a payload query on `DbStatusStore`), the hint Pipeline first and each Pipeline once; a job-lane status falls back to `DbJobRunStore.run` after a restart. A receipt's `diag.msg` is masked on read (`FailureText.scrub` + `SecretScrubber`). An open read like `/runs/{name}/batches` (operator decision on the L4 review; the first cut was gated on `canOperateRuns`). OpenAPI: `ConsignmentTrail` and `StepReceipt` schemas; release note in `okf/product/module/api/plan-api-stability.md`. The ledger read takes the NEWEST row of the id (a drain appends SUCCESS after PARKED) and the newest row per member file; the `run_id` join chunks its `IN` list at 1 000 (PostgreSQL's 65 535-parameter cap), proven on PostgreSQL 18. Tests: `ControlApiConsignmentTrailTest` (11), `DbConsignmentOutputStoreTest` (+3), `DbJobRunStoreTest` (+1 assertion block), `DbStatusStoreTest` (+2), `PostgresSchemaPerSpaceStoresTest` (+2), falsified (`[]` when off → red; `run_id` join dropped → hop test red; and each review fix). Full gate NOT run.

> ↔ **86 (no count change, P2 12, P3 74) on 2026-11-17**: narrowed the §2 gate *X5 cross-lane drill-down + StepInfo envelope* (MoSCoW S4; a §2 row is not counted) — lane **L1** of `archived-documents/plans-archive/2026-11-17-x5-stepinfo-envelope-plan.md` is built. The record is the **Step Receipt** (D7; GLOSSARY entry added, "StepInfo envelope" retired): `StepReceipt` (`@PublicApi` 4.0.0, nested `Outlet` / `Pointer` / `Schema` / `Diagnostics`), whose JSON form is at most 1024 bytes, enforced by the record's constructor (message, then outlets folded into `route:*`, counts, ref path, identity fields, each cut setting `truncated`). `PipelineRel.canonical` maps the legacy reject spellings to `reject:<reason>`, so receipts name outlets one way whether or not L2 lands. `PipelineExecutor.WalkObserver` (default methods; `ProvenanceCollector` adapted onto it, provenance rows unchanged) reports each Step's start, schema and outcome: FAILED with a code at the Step that threw, NOT_REACHED below it. The `consignment_steps` table sits beside `consignment_outputs` in the default-on registry (D3 = c), written once per Consignment (`recordSteps`) and read by `steps(consignmentId)`; `-Dconsignment.steps=false` switches the writes off; `db_maintenance` prunes rows older than `-Dconsignment.steps.retentionDays` (default 30, D10). The at-rest job lane flushes its receipts in a `finally`, success or failure, with conservation imbalances on the Step's `diag`. Not in L1: the ingest lanes, park and drain (L3), the read route (L4: no new route, capability or OpenAPI schema here), and D9's `pipeline.batch.failed` for a failed job-lane run, which the plan's §5 assigns to no lane. Tests: `StepReceiptTest`, `DbConsignmentOutputStoreTest`, `PipelineJobRunnerTest`, each falsified against the plan's mutation. Full gate NOT run.

> ⬇ **89 → 88 (P3 76 → 75) on 2026-11-17**: closed P3 feature *Branch-aware executor residuals* [items: module.engine · kind: feature] (MoSCoW C8). Only when every listed file has a size; one `SIZE_UNKNOWN` (`db` export, Kafka, the `dataset` copy) keeps the cap after landing, as before. No public API signature changed (javadoc only; `public-api.txt` unchanged). New `RemotePreFetchByteCapTest` 7/7, falsified three ways. The file-count cap stays after landing (adaptive and on by default; reopen on an operator call). (d) the flat-home refusals and g2 move to §6 *Pipelines — execution*. As built: `okf/product/module/elt/spec-acquisition.md` § *The poll cycle and its two timers*.
> ↔ **89 (no count change, P2 13, P3 76) on 2026-11-17**: narrowed P3 feature `MODULE-REORG-P4-3` [items: layer.platform · kind: feature] (MoSCoW C4) — switching a module off in a Space now stops its Job Runs in flight and its long-lived subscriptions. `PUT /settings/modules` calls `JobService.cancelSwitchedOffRuns`, which re-reads the Job gate for every executing Run and interrupts the ones of the module's Job Types and maintenance tasks (the `interruptRuns` mechanism of `JOB-OUTLIVES-CLOSE-1`, scoped to those Runs): recorded `CANCELLED` with `module switched off: <the gate's sentence>`, never retried; other modules' Runs and other Spaces untouched. Subscriptions are gated at each event through `ModuleGate.paused` / `ModuleSettings`, not unregistered (no new SPI method): the `exchange` Signal forwarder (`exchange-signal-forward`) and the `geo-link` integration webhook tap (now off when either `geoLink` or `investigations` is). An Action Request dispatch already sending is not interrupted, by decision. Left: Link Analysis graph runs and index builds (`GraphRunService`, `IndexBuildService`). As built in `okf/product/layer/platform/spec-module-reorg-decisions.md`.
> ↔ **89 (no count change, P2 13, P3 76) on 2026-11-17**: narrowed P3 **Platform Services Stage 2 / 3** [items: module.processor · kind: feature · hold: demand] (MoSCoW S6) — the Run-level snapshot shipped: a Job Run pins each Dataset once, at its first `DatasetAccess` read, and every later read in the Run sees the same files across a snapshot swap. The pin hard-links the files into the Run's own directory rather than holding the swap lock, so a publish never waits for a Run; the Run's end (success, failure, cancel) deletes the links. The row keeps filtered `services()` (D4) and the devkit jar (D5), both on demand, so `hold: demand` is back.
> ⬇ **90 → 89 (P3 77 → 76) on 2026-11-17**: closed P3 feature *Consignment addressing* [items: module.engine · kind: feature] (MoSCoW C7). The "ingest-side Consignment-scoped accessor" was never code: it was the option the job-parameter contract deferred on 2026-08-10, an Expression reading the event-time range of a table written by ingest rather than by a Job. Ingest already records those bounds in the output registry, but `$upstream(<job>)` resolves only through a Job's Run and a Collector's ingest is not a Job; no Space, template or screen asks for such a token, so it is not built (reopen trigger: `okf/product/module/elt/spec-consignments.md` §A.5-B). `retire_superseded` stays **off by default**: the task is safe to run (the delete waits for readers through `SnapshotSwapLock.writeAbove` and never deletes a file a `LIVE` row names, now pinned by `MaintenanceLibraryTest.retireSupersededSparesAnAgedFileThatALiveRowStillNames`), but arming it in every Space would make it the only deleting task on by default, and its first pass would delete every superseded revision an install has kept; 3.x kept none, so the 4.0.0 release note tells upgraders to enable a copy of the disabled demo job. Corrected while grounding: `retention_days` counts from a file's write time, not its supersede time, and it is the store lock, not the window, that protects an open read. Not filed: `StreamWatermark` / `producerHighWater` have no production caller (noted in the spec §5).

> ⬇ **89 → 88 (P3 76 → 75) on 2026-11-16** — closed P3 defect `LA-INDEX-SPELLINGS-1` [items: module.la-core, module.la-storage · kind: defect] on the operator's options (b) and (c), not (a): a node stays the raw id everywhere (index buckets, Investigations, Cases, Drafts, aliases). (b) A neighbours read (`POST /inv/projection/neighbors`, the explorer's start and expand) from a value of a column typed as an Entity Type reads every raw spelling of its key, so starting from `ACC-1003` reaches the transfer filed under `' acc-1003 '`; the Dataset path matches by `EntityKeySql` key, the index path folds each raw id the manifest's new optional `spellings` lists for the key (written by every build mode: per type, the keys spelt more than once, at most 10 000 raw ids; `formatVersion` stays 1, no rebuild — an older index reads exactly as before until its next build or append). It falls back to the Dataset when the end is typed under another rule than the build used, the type has too many spellings, or the value is no raw id the index holds. An untyped end matches exactly; under masking each spelling keeps its alias. (c) The import's entity count is `typeNodes` when both ends are typed: 137 on the full-cycle fixture, not 138. Left exact on purpose: the Investigation expand (sealed; LA-17 `merged` is the opt-in), the neighbour list, the Grow preview, recursive paths. Tests `ControlApiOnboardLinkAnalysisTest` (two new, the 137 pinned), `IndexLinkStatsTest`. Docs: `module/la-storage/architecture-la-storage.md` § Spellings of one key, `module/la-core/spec-la-core.md`, `user-guide.md`. Its independent review found the SQL key (`EntityKeySql`) and the Java key disagreeing on full case mappings (`ﬁx-1`: `ﬁX-1` vs `FIX-1`, also `ŉ ǰ ΐ ᾀ ﬀ և` and default `İ`), so such a value's Dataset match — and the four-eyes fan-out count — found none of its own rows and a gated read was served. Fixed in the same lane: `EntityKeySql` folds every Java full case mapping first (the eight inputs now in the shared parity fixture), the Dataset match keeps the raw equality and the index fold keeps the value, so neither falls below the exact match; the listing is complete while it fits (a key that is no raw id is answered by the index), with a 64-spellings-per-key cap. `GET /inv/index` does not show an end typed under another rule since the build (documented). Full gate NOT run.

> ⬇ **88 → 87 (P3 74 → 73) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `JOB-OUTLIVES-CLOSE-1` [items: module.engine · kind: defect]: closing the server did wait for Job Runs, but only cooperatively and only for `SpaceManager`'s 10 s budget; a drain still busy then was abandoned at once and its Run went on writing after `close()` returned (the test's log showed 2–3 Spaces abandoned per run even when it passed; under a second Maven run `t-la-utility` was one, mid-`transformer_loss`). And `sql.template`, which wrote that Dataset, never read the cooperative cancel. Now an overrun drain is escalated before it is abandoned: `CollectorService.interruptRuns()` → `JobService.interruptRuns()` interrupts the in-flight Runs (recorded `CANCELLED`), and the drain gets up to 5 s more, taken from the store flush's budget, so a Space still closes within two budgets; `sql.template` checks the cancel before each write and reveals no snapshot once cancelled. A Run inside a native DuckDB statement still stops only when the statement returns, and is abandoned as before. Tests `SpaceCloseStopsRunsTest`, `JobCloseStopTest`; as-built in the ELT hub's architecture (§E6 *Stopping a Space*).

> ⬇ **88 → 87 (P3 74 → 73) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `LA-UTILITY-LOOKALIKE-CHECKS-1` [items: solution-pack.la-utility · kind: defect] (the 2026-11-07 showcase plan's two known gaps): (1) the prepaid look-alike, supermarket vendor `V-01`, now sells in Coventry, the fraud's city (`tools/gen-la-utility-data.py`, seed 20261107, regenerated byte-identically; its top-ups are 10/20/20 so it stays the busiest vendor), so `UtilityLinksJobGoldenTest`'s no-link check has a real choice to check: `V-01` shares one ordinary customer (`PRM-0182`) with `V-08` and none of the 40. (2) Decided: two hops is acceptable. A story is its hub's links, and a busy contractor (`C-27`, `C-18`), a transformer or a shared customer puts most premises two hops from most others; keeping look-alikes further out would plant an emptiness no real grid has. What changed instead: `noLookAlikeLinksToAnyStory` checks every look-alike against EVERY story's entities (not only its own), so a two-hop path always runs through an ordinary entity, and the new `aLookAlikeStandsBesideItsStoryTwoHopsAwayThroughAnOrdinaryEntity` pins the three stories whose look-alike stands two hops away and `V-01` sharing a customer with `V-08`. Falsified: the old data plus a genuine reconnection visited by `C-14` fails both tests; the new data with one fraud token sold at `V-01` fails the no-link test. Hub user guide, admin guide and `la-showcase` architecture updated (loss now 6.0 → 17.8 % on TX-007, 4.6 → 24.7 % on TX-019).
> ⬇ **89 → 88 (P3 75 → 74) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `LA-TELCO-RA-FIRST-BOOT-RESOLVED-ALERT-1` [items: solution-pack.la-telco-ra, addon.incidents · kind: defect]: not a race and not the Alert semantics, the shipped data. A first boot lands each monthly feed one file at a time, in month order, and the counts Job runs between them; while a month is the latest landed, a late-billed look-alike (its first two months never billed, a provisioning lag) meets the rule exactly as the story does, until its third month lands billed. The generator drew the 12 lags from activations of 2025-11..2026-06, so some ended before the story's months (2026-03 on): replaying the counts Job's own SQL over every landing state (u usage months × b billing months landed) on the old data reads 1, 4, 0, 1, 302 … 300 along the diagonal, which is the observed fire at 4, heal at 0, fire at 1, refresh to 300. Fixed in `tools/gen-la-telco-ra-data.py`: the lags are drawn from activations of 2026-03..2026-06, so the count stays 0 until the story's second month and then reads 300 to 306 down to 300, never 0; regenerated (only billing rows, two top-up rows and the ground truth's `late_billing` / `dropped_months` ids changed; 12, 50, 300 and 40 unchanged). Not changed, by design: the rule (2 or more unbilled months, still unbilled in the latest judged one: the spec's "over one period") and the general heal on the first healthy sweep — a lag that is billed later IS healed live; `healAfterSweeps` was rejected because every terminal batch is a sweep, so it would count batches, not months. `TelcoRaLinksJobGoldenTest.onAFirstBootTheCountNeverFallsBackToZeroWhileTheMonthsLand` pins every interleaving of the two feeds (fails on the old data at 1 with 3 + 3 months landed, and on the relapse after 4 + 5). The boot-test is not repeated; out-of-order months within a feed are not covered.

Already right on master: the saved-view toast's curly quotes, *Industry pack*, and the D-2 banner no longer says *physicalRef*. Left (operator): the embed host-flag hint, *Onboard a file*, *capabilities*, the link index version, and the showcase Dataset descriptions.
> ⬇ **90 → 89 (P3 76 → 75) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `LA-RADIAL-LAYOUT-SQUASH-1` [items: module.la-core · kind: defect] (Basic UI test of 2026-11-10, D-5): the Radial layout is now `component-radial` (`inspecto/graph/graph-radial-layout.ts`, registered beside the sliced layouts; `layoutConfig('radial')` passes the drawn node size and a spacing of 0.75 × node × the Display spacing). G6's own `radial` centred on the FIRST node in the data, held the nodes of one ring apart by its fixed `linkDistance` (50) however wide the ring, assumed 10 px nodes in its overlap pass, and put another component on one more ring with made-up distances: measured on the D-5 graph (V-001 and 24 neighbours, plus a pair) at Link Analysis' Small / Wide start, the neighbours covered 34° with two nodes 10 px apart. Now each connected component is a breadth-first tree round its best-connected node (the data's order breaks a tie), each node owning a share of the circle in proportion to the leaves below it, each ring at least one unit radius out and widened until no two of its nodes come closer than node size + spacing; the components are packed in rows, the largest first. The same graph: 345° (24 evenly round V-001), every two nodes at least 80 px apart (node + spacing), the pair laid out beside it. Pure and O(nodes + links); a link that closes a cycle is drawn but places no node. Not changed, by design: the picked layout stays chosen for the next graph (`pickedLayout` is the analyst's presentation choice, like node size and spacing, undoable and saved with a view; an over-cap or tree-only pick is still dropped), and the Radial cap (`display.radial_max_nodes`, 100) stays. Not driven in a browser (the local demo backend is too old to draw graphs): proven by `graph-radial-layout.spec.ts`, on the pure layout and through G6 5.1.1's own layout controller.
> ⬇ **91 → 90 (P3 77 → 76) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `DEFAULT-SPACE-LOOKUP-STEP-NO-JOB-1` [items: module.config, module.elt · kind: defect]: the default Space's `lookup_step` gets `config/jobs/lookup_step_rollup_job.toon` (`type: pipeline`, `pipeline_config: lookup_step/lookup_step_pipeline.toon`, `on_pipeline: lookup_step`), shaped exactly like its five siblings', so its chain runs at rest into `lookup_step_rollup` and a save no longer warns `WARN_CHAIN_WITHOUT_PIPELINE_JOB`. A Job, not a removed chain, because the lookup is meant to run at rest: `PipelineLift.stageTwo` lifts a `lookup` (among Steps it refuses only `route` and a pre-parse filter), the Step is an inline static map that needs no reference, the `07-steps/lookup` example it mirrors ships `lookup_rollup_job.toon`, and the Space has carried a lookup sample since 2026-09-22; the 2026-09-06 commit that added `lookup_step` added the example's Job but not the Space's. Proved by running the committed Job over the landed sample (`DemoCorpusIngestTest`: 6 rows, NEW/SHIPPED/CANCELLED → Open/Done/Void, RETURNED passed through) and by `/validate` of every chained default-Space Pipeline with the Space's Jobs hosted (`ControlApiChainWithoutJobTest`; red on `lookup_step` alone before the Job). `seed-inbox.sh|ps1` still do not seed `lookup_step` (`tools/seed-samples.mjs` does).

> ⬇ **93 → 92 (P3 79 → 78) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `PIPELINE-CHAIN-NO-JOB-1` [items: module.config, module.elt · kind: defect]: a Pipeline with a chain (a `steps:` entry, or `processing.dedup` / `summarize` / `profile` / `join`) and an `output_store:` that no enabled `type: pipeline` Job runs now saves with `WARN_CHAIN_WITHOUT_PIPELINE_JOB` at `output_store`, naming the fix (add a `type: pipeline` Job that runs `<pipeline>`). Always a warning, active or draft, never a refusal (the Job is a separate config). A Job counts when its `pipeline_config:` resolves to a file whose registered id is the Pipeline's, not by file name: the shipped samples name `orders_pipeline.toon` for `STEP_DEDUP`. Judged at every `SaveGate` door that knows the Space's Jobs, `/validate` included; not on a bundle import or a template seed; a Job save or delete does not re-judge Pipelines. As built: `okf/product/module/config/spec-config-safety.md`, "A chain with no output store".
> ⬇ **94 → 93 (P3 80 → 79) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `SPACE-PROMOTION-CASE-PAIRING-1` [items: module.config · kind: defect]: a promotable bundle path that names, as the filesystem resolves it (`ImportCapabilityGuard.normalizedPath`), a target file or another bundle path spelled otherwise (`Registry/datasets/d.toon` against `registry/datasets/d.toon`) is refused by preview and apply, 422 `caseCollisions`, nothing written — it was an add plus a remove, and with `removals: true` the remove deleted what the add wrote. Refusing was chosen over pairing by the resolved path, which would still have to pick the spelling to write and refuse an ambiguous target (`a.toon` beside `A.toon`); refusing names no spelling and an export never collides with its own Space (refusing every name not in lower case was not an option: a name may carry upper case). The two edges: only a file a loader reads, as spelled, changes what is live (`SpacePromotion.liveConnection` / `liveJob`), so writing `x_connection.TOON` registers nothing and removing `foo_job.TOON` no longer stops the live Job `foo` nor revokes its approvals. As built: [space promotion §2](../okf/product/module/config/spec-space-promotion.md), "A path spelled in another case".
> ⬇ **95 → 94 (P3 81 → 80) on 2026-11-15** — closed P3 defect `IMPORT-CONNECTION-CASE-1` [items: module.config · kind: defect]: the export reads a connection's name as the import does (`ImportCapabilityGuard.normalizedPath`), so `GET /export` strips the literal secret of a hand-made `connections/x_connection.TOON`, `x_CONNECTION.toon` or `Registry/connections/x.toon` that `POST /import` wrote (until now it travelled verbatim), and a Space Template's `x_connection.TOON` is validated as any connection (`TemplateSeedGate`). The row's premise was half right: three of its five sites (`BundleImporter.disableForMissingConnections`, `DecisionRuleGuard.carriedConnections`, the import's registration in `DataSourceRoutes`) decide what goes **live**, and the loaders read names as spelled (`ServiceBootstrap.resolveBySuffix`), so reading them lower-cased would register a connection a restart drops, keep a Pipeline active on it, and let an invoke-api Decision Rule name it; they keep the spelling, now commented. The import had no literal-secret exclusion to fix: it writes a connection's bytes whatever its case. As built: [space promotion §2](../okf/product/module/config/spec-space-promotion.md), "How a file's kind is read".

(C) the save names every Step of a chain on a Pipeline with no top-level `output_store:` (a `steps:` entry, or `processing.dedup` / `summarize` / `profile` / `join`): `ERR_CHAIN_WITHOUT_OUTPUT_STORE` 422 when active, `WARN_CHAIN_WITHOUT_OUTPUT_STORE` on an inactive draft. The row's premise was half right: an ACTIVE store-less chain was already refused at the save by `ConfigSpecs`' `stage-two-blocks-require-output-store` rule (no code, Step not named) and at arming by `requireRunnable`; an inactive draft saved with no word. Not covered: a chain WITH `output_store:` but no `type: pipeline` Job still saves clean and never runs. As built: `okf/product/module/config/spec-config-safety.md`, "A chain with no output store".

> ⬇ **111 → 110 (P3 91 → 90) on 2026-11-13**: closed P3 defect `JOB-LOADER-SAVED-VIEW-SUFFIX-1` [items: module.engine, solution-pack.la-logistics · kind: defect] — the boot suffix scan (`ServiceBootstrap.resolveBySuffix`, every `*_job.toon` / `*_enrich.toon` / `*_meta.toon` / ops-config scan) no longer enters a walked directory's `registry/`, the component store's tree, so `la-logistics`' saved view `driver_team_inside_job.toon` is no longer read as a Job and the boot WARN is gone. > ⬆ **110 → 111 (P3 90 → 91) on 2026-11-13**: filed P3 defect `EVENT-JOURNAL-TORN-UTF8-1` [items: module.event · kind: defect], found by the `LA-PERF-N9-1` verification.
> ⬇ **111 → 110 (P3 91 → 90) on 2026-11-13**: closed P3 defect `LA-PERF-N9-1` [items: module.la-api · kind: defect] — the event store's Parquet flush no longer runs on the request thread or holds the store lock while it writes. The journal write stays synchronous, so an audit event is durable before the reply. The flush rotates the journal into a segment, writes the batch on its own DuckDB connection, then moves the files into place under the lock. Replay reads segments. Per-append p99 fell from 153–245 ms to 5.7–7.3 ms. Plain option (c) (async flush, still under the lock) was measured first and gave no gain. The post-boot projection tail is accepted as a cold-start cost (operator, 2026-11-13). As built: `okf/product/module/event/architecture-event.md` §9 and [`la-performance-plan`](plans-archive/la-performance-plan.md) §0b N9.

The per-Job `TriggerCoalescer` is gone from `JobService` (its drain only queued the Run, so it never held a Signal back). `JobSignalCoalesceTest` failed on the old code with the live symptom. The showcase `cron: "*/5 * * * *"` fallbacks stay as harmless catch-ups. As built: `module/processor/spec-jobs.md`.

> ↔ **70 (no count change) on 2026-11-06**: filed and closed P2 defect `BASIC-EVENTS-DURABLE-1` [items: edition.basic, module.event, module.processor · kind: defect] — Basic live check: the audit anchor roll failed in every Space ("the audit chain no longer holds the record the last anchor names"). Not a chain break: Basic ran the in-memory event store, whose chain restarts at seq 1 on every boot, so an anchor (its own, or one Preview wrote over the shared Spaces' Parquet chain) named a record the next boot never held. Operator decision: Basic is durable — every edition's `serve.*` passes `-Devents.backend=parquet` (`offerings/basic.toon`, `tools/check-launchers.mjs`); anchors are never written over a memory store and carry a MAC'd `store` tag; a memory-era anchor file gets ONE automatic signed break under five conditions (decision A; accepted cost recorded in the event hub). `AuditAnchorsDurableRestartTest` 8/8, mutation-checked.

Both refusals now name the server's local day, and the UI shows every server-recorded time through one formatter that turns a zoned value to local time (event dates untouched); wire fields stay ISO UTC. > ↔ **73 (no count change) on 2026-11-05**: filed and closed P3 feature `ADD-DATA-ROW-FILTER-1` [items: edition.basic, module.processor, module.elt · kind: feature]. No-code row filtering in Add data (DT-7, Basic): on the review step a person adds row conditions on a column (is, is not, contains, a from / to range for numbers and dates), and all of them must hold. The commit sends them as `filter: [{column, op, value | min | max}]`; the server checks them (a refused condition is 422 naming it) and writes them as the import Pipeline's `csv_settings.where`, rendered from typed values with text quoted, so no raw value reaches SQL. Every later file is filtered by the same Pipeline (Add a file, Run now, the inbox poll, a Postgres snapshot refresh). The Dataset card says "Keeps rows where …", and the run summary and each arrival say how many rows the filter left out (not counted for JSON / NDJSON / Excel). This closes item (6) of `LA-ONBOARD-RESIDUALS-1`. Editing the conditions after the commit is `ADD-DATA-FILTER-EDIT-1`. As built: `okf/product/module/processor/api-onboarding-authoring.md` ("Keep only some rows"), `okf/product/module/la-core/architecture-data-onboarding.md` §6.10.

> ⬇ **73 → 72 (P3 60 → 59) on 2026-11-01**: closed P3 feature `OIDC-CC-MACHINE-ACTOR-1` (review G7). A client-credentials token — `gty` `client_credentials` (or Auth0's `client-credentials`), or a client listed in `-Dauth.oidc.clients` — becomes a machine Subject: its id is `client:<client id>` (`azp`, else `client_id`), so the audit actor and every owner field name the integration; `actor_type` is `service`; the token's `sub` (a Keycloak service-account UUID) rides on each audit row as `idp_subject`. > ⬇ **74 → 73 (P2 14 → 13) on 2026-11-01**: closed P2 defect `OPSTORE-BULK-INSERT-1` — on DuckDB `DbDedupLedger.claim` is an Appender into a TEMP staging table plus ONE `INSERT … SELECT DISTINCT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING` (the won set), `DbStatusStore.sync` appends inside its transaction, and the windowed dedup's lost-key table uses the Appender too; Postgres keeps its statements; `DbEventStore.append` writes one event per call and stays. > ⬇ **78 → 76 (P3 64 → 62) on 2026-11-01**: closed P3 features `OIDC-CC-CLIENT-PIN-1` and `OIDC-CC-LIFETIME-REPLAY-1` (review G1, G2, G4, G6 and the 5b note). Opt-in `-Dauth.oidc.clients` pins the Bearer path to the listed machine clients plus the SPA client (`azp`, else `client_id`; none → 401), and turning it on refuses boot without `auth.oidc.audience` or with a non-https non-loopback JWKS. `auth.oidc.maxTokenLifetime` (ISO-8601, `iat` required) and `auth.oidc.refuseReplay` (per-node `jti` cache until `exp` + 60 s, requires the ceiling) apply to listed machine clients only, so people's tokens are unchanged; either without `clients` refuses boot. Not built: sender-constrained tokens (DPoP / mTLS), and the gateway path is not pinned. As-built: `okf/product/module/oidc/spec-oidc.md` (`MachineTrust`), admin guide section 5; `OidcMachineTrustTest`.
> ⬇ **79 → 78 (P3 65 → 64) on 2026-11-01**: closed P3 chore `SPACE-DATA-ROOT-CONFIG-AUDIT-1` — operator decision 2026-11-01: move to the data root. LA snapshots and Investigations (`audit/snapshots/`), LA upload records and the quota ledger (`audit/la-uploads/`) and the identity fact log (`audit/entity-facts/`) open their directory through `SpaceAuditDir`: `<data root>/audit/<dir>` when the Space has a data root (the `PathJail.onDataRoot` redirect), `config/audit/<dir>` unchanged otherwise. The first open moves the old files across once, never overwriting (a file in both places stays in both with a WARN; the new one is read); a read that misses the new place re-runs the move, then falls back to the old file. As-built: `okf/product/module/config/architecture-config.md`.

Fix: the route resolves a `masked:` alias to the scored key it tokenises (after the data-scope gate; RowScope judged on the resolved key; a 404 names only the alias; the key is masked on read as before). `ControlApiRiskScoreTest` reads by alias over real HTTP (200 for an analyst, raw key absent; revealer sees it; unknown alias 404). An Investigation canvas masks with its own key and stays open as the residual.

> ⬇ **60 → 59 (P3 43 → 42) on 2026-10-13**: closed P3 `LA-INDEXED-RESTRICTED-EXPAND-1` — a restricted `expand` (`only`) is now answered from the link index: `IndexedExpand` cuts to the chosen neighbours BEFORE the per-anchor fan-out rank, exactly as the flat CTE's `other IN only`, so the cap, truncation and budget count only the chosen ones. Measured first (DuckDB 1.5.5, 20 M-row parquet with a planted 500-contact hub): flat one-hop read ~740 ms restricted or not; adjacency lookup ~8 ms unrestricted / ~17 ms restricted, ~40-90x. `ControlApiInvIndexedExpansionTest.aRestrictedExpandSealsTheSameRowsFromTheIndexAndFromTheFlatDataset` pins index rows/fingerprint/delta equal to flat (directions, minEvents, fan-out cap fired, budget). The `list` form of `/inv/projection/neighbors` was ALREADY index-served (`IndexedNeighbors`, D-3 step 6) — the row's premise was stale there.
